AWARD 
This is a caso taken under Section 2A ( 2 ) of the I. D . Act , 
1947 in view of the judgment of the Hon ble High Court of 
Andhra Pradesh reported in W . P . No . 8395 of 1989 dated 
3 - 8 - 1995 between Sri U . Chinnappa and M / s . Cotton Corpo 
ration of India and two others . 

2 . Brief averments of the petition aie : That the petitioner 
WHS appointed in thọ year 1989 45 his father took voluntary 
retirement due to sickness. The petitioner was working at 
Hemachandrapuram Mines attending his duties regularly . On 
25- 7 -90 his older brother was ,murdered by unsocial elements 
and the petitionor was also grievously injured in the attack 
with the result the petitioner was not able to attend his duties . 
And this fact is well known to the management as this incident 
was also written in the newspaper. The unsocial elements 
having murdered the elder orother of the petitioner has been 
trying to eliminatc the petitioner as he was eye witness, AS 
such the petitioner had to seek hide out at Warangal, where 
he fell ill due to the injuries and mental agony. The petitioner 
returned for duty and he was laken , on duty on 8 - 5 -91 levelling 
the charge of unauthorized absentceísni, in response to which 
ho gavo 4 detailed explanation . The respondent without giving 
due consideration, dismissed the petitioner from services 
through order No. GM ( K ) / P7/ 1713 duted 21- 9 -91, violating 
the principlos of natural justice , in result, the order of icigoval . 
passed by the respondent is illegal, invalid , and not sustainable 
in law . The absence from duty wiis beyond petitioner s control. 
He was waiting for years but to no use . Hence , it is prayed 
that the dismissal order dated 21- 9 -91 be set aside and the 
petitioner may be directed to be reinstated . 

3 . A counter was filed stating that petitioner was appointeri 
as a temporary Tunnel Mazdoor on 9 - 1 -89 on the basis of 
dependent employment and the particulars of the musters 
attended by the petitioner are as under : 

1989 210 musters 
1990 69 musters 

1991 Upto June 30 musters. 
As such , he was issued a notice dated 5 - 7 - 91 . A icply was 
given by the petitioner on 6 - 7 -91 and not satisfied with the 
reply and enquiry was conducted and he was dismissed from 
service on 24 - 9 - 91. That suddenly after 10 years the petitioner 
sought to reopen the issue I gave legal notice dated 
21 - 7 - 2001. The enquiry was conducted fairly and properly . It 
is false to say that petitioner filed any appeal and there is a 
long delay of 10 years . He is not entitled for any relief . 
Hence, the petition may be dismissed . 

4 . The Counsel for the petitioner conceded that the domes 
tic enquiry is validly conducted and Ex. Wi to Ex . W5 are 
marked on his behalf and Ex. Mi to Ex , M8 were marked 
with consent on behalf of Respondent. And, arguments were 
heard on the main issue.

ORDER 
This case M . P No. 2199 is transferred from Labour Court 
ifr, Hyderabad in view of the Government of India , Ministry 
Of Labour s order No. H - 11026 / 1 /2001- IR ( C - II) dated 
18 - 10 - 2001 and renumbered in this Court as M . P . No. 
9 /2001. 

2 . This is a petition filed by Sri Khæja Moinuddin and 12 
others under Section 33 C ( 2 ) rc. with Sec . 5 of 1. D . Act. 

3 . The brief averments in the petition are ; That the watch 
men working with the respondent. They have initially appoint 
ed on daily wages basis ind continued on daily wages from 
1976 to 1981 wherein their daily wage was Rs. 4 per day . 
During the above said period a regular permanent, employed 
was paid a basic salary of Rs. 210 per month excluding other 
allowances. As per the 1, D , Act, the casual worker is entitled 
for a wage equal to 1 /30th of the basic wage of the icgular 
employec per day plus dearness allowance which was 179 
per cent of the wage : Is petitioner entitled to the amount 
shown as per calculation memo ? During the above sajd period 
the petitioner were not paid for the weekly offs and public 
holidays . Hence, the respondents have to pay arrears of 
wages and weekly off wages to the petitioners which is their 
legal right. Hence, the Hon ble Court may be ploase to 
direct the respondents to pay Vic Num of Rs. 2 , 29 , 511.05 
towards arrears . 

4 . The respondent filed a counter filed stating that the 
appropriate Govt. for the Respondent is the Central Govt . 
Labour Court only is the proper Court for the purpose of 
entertaining M . T . under Sec . 33 C ( 2 ) of the I. D . Act. There . 
fore the present petition is not maintainable and tho Court 
has no jurisdiction . ( It was filed before the Labour Court- TIT 
transferred to this Tribunal on constitution ). It is not sub 
mitted that the petitioner originally engaged as watchman on 
daily wages depending . lipon the avallubility of work for 
the purpose of safeguarding food grains . Food grains in the 
open storage units and hired depots . Their engagement was 
never continuous , They only work during the days and periods 
mentioned in annexure enclosed with counter. The allegations 
that they worked continuously from 1976 to 1981 is not 
correct. During the period , they worked on daily wages . 
They were paid minimum wages as prescribed by the Govt. 
of A . P . in Its G . O .M . S . No . 549 dated 3 -6 - 1976 . As per the 
said G . O . minimum monthly wage prescribed for watchman 
is Rs. 102. 70ps. for Zone -II, Nalgonda District falls under 
Zone- II. The above said wage was divided by 26 days and 
the daily wage arrived at on the above principle was paid 
to the potitioncs. Hence , the 4 monthly wage was divided 
by 26 days instead of 30 days , the daily wage paid to the 
petitioner had taken care of the weekly off. Accordingly , the 
petitioners were paid Rs. 4 per day . It is submitted that the 
netitioners were given regular appointment hetween the vears 
1991 and 1993 and from the date of thir regularization they 
were on regular scale of pay and were paid all other benefits 
payable to the regular employees of the Food Corporation 
of India . As prior to regularization they were only daily wage 
workers . They are not entitled to regular scale of pay and 
cannot be treated on par with regular employ 
ees. Furthor they are not entitled to wage of Rs. 19 .53 
payable to the regular cmoluvees . Further the claim 
is abnormally delayed ind it is liable t he disinissed 
on the sole grounds . They have not based on their 
claim under any provision . Hence , they cannot claim these 
benofits and the said claims cannot be adjudicated under Sec. 
33 C ( 2 ) of the J. D . Act. The M . P . is therefore no maintain 
able and the same is accordingly table to be dismissed . Hence, 
it is liable to be dismissel in limini.

10 . It is argued by the Learned Counsel for the petitioner 
that actually number of working days put in by the petitioner 
are on record and WW1 has spoken to the same they are not 
in dispute. It is reported in AIR 1978 SC 8 Delhi Cloth and 
General Mills Ltd . Vs. Sambhu Nath Mukerjee , it was held 
that removal of name from muister rolls constitutes to illegal 
retrenchment and the workman is entitled to reinstatement 
with continuity of service and full back wages from the date 
of illegal retrenchment. 

11 . Even , assuming that he was engaged as contract jabour 
it is submitted that under the Contract Labour Act, the em 
ployer is required to obtain license from the Labour com 
missioner concerned . The contractor who supplies the contract 
labour should have registered his name in the office as 
contractor and obtain license to supply contract labour . The 
respondent management did not addtice any evidence to 
establish that it is registered itself to be able to engage 
contract labour. There is also no evidence on record to 
establish that the persons who stvled themselves as contracts 
are entitled to supnly contract labour . In fact there was no 
mention of any contractor s nance fulfilling the prescribed 
conditions. The local officers , who retired from services posed 
as suppliers of contract labeur without the authoritv to do so . 
It was in fact camoufiage of the local ofñcers who engage 
the casual labour , obviously that the connivance of higher 
authorities. A reference to the Judgement of the 5 - Judge 
Bench of the Supreme Court reported in 2001 SCC ( L & S ) 1121 
establishes the said fart. It may he noted that the workman 
in continuous service for more than 240 days in anch vent 
commencing from his initial anrointment. An acerunt of 
malpractices like the workman are being sadistically harassed 
and victimized . Therefore , this Court may be pleased to 
consider his record of service and declare that he was illegally 
retrenched from service and he is entitled to be reinstated 
with continuity of servire and back wages .

6 . Admittedly , this factory is registered 
under the Factory Act, and complies with rules 
and regulatory provisions under the said Act, 
to the extent not specifically exempted . No 
doubt, the manufacturing related to defence 
without profit motive but it does not mean 
that the activities of the factory is of sovereign 
nature , rather, the ordnance factory dis 
charges corporate functions, and so , it is held 
to be an industry . Legal opinion relied by 
the einployees can not over rule judicial 
decision . 


9 . In workmen of American Express Inter 
national Banking Corporation Vs. Manage 
ment of American Express International Bank 
ing Corporation 1985 (51) FLR 481, the Apex 
Court held " that section 25 - F of the I. D . Act, 
is plainly intended to give relief to retrenched 
workmen . The expression which we are re 
quired to construe " actually worked under 
the einployer” . The expression , according to 
us can not mean those days only when the 
workinan worked with hammer, sickle or pen , 
butmust nosessarily comprehend all liose days 
during which he was in the employment of 
the employer and for which he had been paid 
wages either under express or implied contract 
of service or by compulsion of statute , stand 
ing order etc .” 


7 . The management relies on direction of 
the Director General vide letter No . 75 76 
Misc . ANI dated 19th Sept. 1995 relating 
to employment of sweepers, which makes 
mandatory that only those engaged on nerrick 
rates could be regularised , if had put 240 
days service in a year for two years conti 
nuously or more and had been sponsored by 
the local Employment Exchange . This leiter 
take cognizance of 240 days service in a year 
as per provision of the I. D . Act: which 
implies that the Director General, Ordnance 
was alive that 240 days service in i year 
constitute " continuous service " under seciion 
25 -B of the I. D . Act and so ; for purposes of 
regularisation , emphasis on two years conti 
nuous service was laid . The submission that 
Ordnance Factory , Dehradun is not an 
Industry is belied by the stand of the Director 
General, Oddnance . The provisions of I. D . 
Act, are applicable in the Ordnance Factory , 
Dehradun also . 


10 . Mrs. Rolley Mahendra admitted not 
adding Sunday lying in between the working 
days or other authorised leave. No record is 
produced to explain the situation as what 
were the reasons therefore , whether payments 
were made and if not, whether there xisted 
any direction . The workman was entitled to 
weekly res : day and there is nothing on record 
to show her unwillingness to work during her 
association for 4 years after heing duly sel- ct 
ed . The direction of the Director General 
Ordnance , was to regulate those having work 
ed for 240 days in a vear for two years and 
not to dispense with the services without benefit 
under section 25 - F or 25 - N of the I. D . Act. 


11. The action of the management wishout 
notice, votice pay and retrenchment comrers ? 
tion was void and the workman is entitleri , 
reinstaternent. If actual working periori is 
calcillater 29 per law laid down by the Apex 
Court, she would be entitled to regularisatio ? 
also as per direction of the Director Genral. 
Ordnance . 


12 . Accordingly , the award is : 


8 . Whether Smt. Kamla Devi worked for 
more than 240 days in a vear to bring her Case 
within the ambit of section 25 - F or 25 - N of 
the J. D . Act. It is admitted by the MW , Mrs. 
Rolley Mahendra that Sunday and holi ( lays 
were not added in calculating 240 days " actual 
service " in a year . This statement creates 
doubt in calculating working days and the 
chart filed by the management. The chart has 
been prepared calander vear -wise and not 
year-wise from the date of joining , as per the 
direction of the Director General, Ordnance 
which mnentions 240 days in a year and rot 
in a calander vear . In any event, non -inclu 
sion of Sundays and other holidavs in actual 
working davs made the calculation wrong. 
Thare should have heen calculation Ver -wise 
and not calander vear -wise. Accepting the 
chart of working days as it is relied hy the 
maņgument, the workman worked for 258 112 
days in the vear 1991, and her case is cover 
ed by section 25 - B of the I. D . Act,
